04/08/2024, 20:42 - Irineos Livadiotes: *Wedding invitation* 

Marios and Ariadne are getting married on *Sunday 18th August at 2pm* after Divine Liturgy in Chapel Street Church with a *reception following from 4pm at Edinburgh Academy (54 Henderson Row, Stockbridge)* and they want to let everyone know they are all welcome to both.
They kindly ask if people would help cater by bringing food to the reception for everyone to share. They would love if people could prepare hot or cold foods and maybe a few desserts.
The Venue is *nut free* so they ask that no dishes contain nuts in them.
Some drinks will be provided at the reception, but it would also be appreciated if people can bring their own either to have or to share.
Please contact Ariadne on 07810 162818 if you can provide food.
Food can be brought to the venue from 11am on the day or as you arrive at the venue. 
With love and Prayers Marios and Ariadne.

28/08/2024, 19:32 - Irineos Livadiotes: <Media omitted>
*Pilgrimage to St Ninian's cave Saturday 21/09/2024*

Our Holy Church celebrates our Righteous Father Among the Saints, St Ninian on 16th of September each year. We are organising a full day pilgrimage close to his feast day to his Cave by the sea and Monastery in Whithorn (South West Coast of Scotland).

*God willing and weather permiting this is how the day is to go*
When: Saturday 21/09/2024
Departure: 7am from Meadow Lane 
Arrival at assigned parking for Cave: 10.15am
Akathist to St Ninian (by the Cave):11.00am
Lunch: 12.30pm (Town hall)
Visit to Monastery remains: 14.15pm
Return: 15.00pm for 18.15pm arrival in Edinburgh. 

🚗🚕🚗🚕
We would travel there and back by private cars and all participants are expected to contribute towards the fuel of the drivers and the town hall hire. 

*Expected contributions*

Car drivers £0
Car passengers £15
Full car parties (families and in any other way full car parties): £5 (contribution for hall hire)

If you for any reason your contribution is to be different or £0 please speak to Irineos. The contribution amount should not be stopping you from participating. 

*Please join us!*
For participating, please speak to Irineos (07805733352) or if you can't reach him please speak to Alina as soon as possible.
Please clarify if you are a driver and will be driving your car on the day, passenger or let us know you will be coming in full car party/family.

24/04/2025, 09:31 - +44 7798 776427: Community Garden Project – Call for Volunteers!

Dear all,

We’re excited to announce a new community initiative at Craigmillar Park Church to help beautify the grounds and create a peaceful, welcoming space for everyone.

Our purpose is to plant flowers and herbs at the back of Craigmillar Park Church, under the direction of a professional gardener Robin and with the blessing of our Bishop, to enhance the natural beauty of the space and help prevent weeds from regrowing too easily.

If you’re interested in getting involved, please join this group! https://chat.whatsapp.com/IOfRXOV6VhsI2uSR08JtZy

We’ll be coordinating efforts here and sharing updates. No gardening experience is required—just a willingness to help and enjoy some fresh air and fellowship.

Saturdays are looking like the ideal day for our planting days. Robin, our gardener, will help guide us in what to plant, when, and where. Our community will be providing consumables like compost, tools, and access to the garden shed, as well as a pair of hands for the heavier work.

Please don’t hesitate to get in touch if you have questions—or green thumbs! I’ll also be inviting Robin to this group shortly.

Let’s grow something beautiful together.

Warm regards,
Martina Kyriazi Whitton
Community Admin

05/05/2025, 02:08 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear brothers and sisters in Christ,

We are planning our Community’s Annual General Meeting (AGM) for Sunday 11th May at 1:30 pm at 33 Chapel Street.

All parishioners are invited to attend the meeting in order to be informed about current activities, issues and aims of our Community. Reports by clergy and by lay members of Community groups will be followed by an opportunity for those present to ask questions and contribute to a discussion aiming to further the objectives of the Community.

By the grace of God and through the prayers and selfless labours of our clergy and all who devotedly assist them, the numbers in our congregation have increased significantly. Along with the joy in the growth of our Community, there are legal implications to this, due to which we were advised to consider altering our charitable status from an unincorporated Scottish charity to a Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO). Since the last AGM, the committee has made significant progress on this, and we are now ready to present the draft SCIO constitution to the AGM for endorsement.

At the end of the meeting, there will be elections for members of the church committee. The election of committee members is done by the registered Community members present at the AGM.

Registered Community members are invited to nominate up to seven candidates for the church committee, having first ascertained the willingness of each to serve. The deadline for submitting nominations is 23:59 on Thursday 8th May. Nominations should be sent to the Secretary, Antigoni Mitsioni, at the following email address: mantigon@msn.com

The following extract from the Constitution explains the conditions of membership of the Community:

ARTICLE III – Members

The members of the Orthodox Church in Scotland are all those of whatever age, sex or nationality, who have been duly accepted into Communion with the Holy Orthodox Patriarchs and are resident in Scotland.

The members of the Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew are those of the above as defined at III(i), who worship in the Edinburgh church, who accept the spiritual jurisdiction and authority of the Clergy of the Community, the Archdiocese of Thyateira and Great Britain, and the Patriarchate of Constantinople, and who abide by this Constitution. Members entitled to vote at meetings of the Community shall be those who have reached their eighteenth birthday and who, for at least the six months previous to their exercising such a vote, have attended services of the Community in Edinburgh or elsewhere, and who have paid an annual subscription of an amount to be determined from time to time by a General Meeting
The entire (current) constitution document is accessible via the following link: https://edinburghorthodox.files.wordpress.com/2023/04/constitution-of-the-edinburgh-orthod ox-community-of-st-andrew-2023.pdf
 

Membership fee
The membership fee, set by previous AGMs at £20 for employed people and at £10 for students and the unemployed, must be paid annually to the treasurer in order for parishioners to obtain or renew their membership status in line with the law governing charities in this country (our Community is a registered charity with Scottish Charity Number: SC040613).

For regular members of the parish who find it difficult for financial reasons to pay the above annual subscription, the Community will partly or fully cover it by means of a donation, so that finance should not be an obstacle to parishioners who wish to register and vote at the AGM on matters concerning our Community.

Agenda:
Prayer
Introduction
Minutes of the last AGM
Reports: 
Clergy
Chair of committee
Treasurer
Sunday School
Greek School
Community’s youth fellowship
Draft SCIO constitution (accessible via this link): proposal and endorsement process
Any other business
Elections for new committee
Prayer

Thank you, 
Matthaios Tsolakis, Chairman of St Andrew's Church Committee.

15/05/2025, 11:26 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear brothers and sisters in Christ,

Christ is risen!

At our Community's Annual General Meeting last Sunday (11 May), the following was decided by means of a majority vote of 52 out of 53 registered members present:
Approval of the draft SCIO constitution as it stood, in order to proceed with the application to OSCR. This was deemed to be a priority, given the risk of financial liability to both Committee and Community members under our Community’s current unincorporated charitable status, as compared to the removal of financial liability under the incorporated charitable status the above application aims to achieve.
To have an Extraordinary General Meeting (EGM) in order to give an opportunity to Community members who felt they had not had enough time to study the draft SCIO constitution carefully to make any proposals for amendments and/or additions.
Any proposals for amendments and/or additions to the new constitution should be sent by email to Antigoni Mitsioni (mantigon@msn.com) by 23:59 on Thursday 29 May 2025 (two weeks’ notice).
Following the above cut-off time, if there are any proposals for amendments and/or additions to the new constitution, these will be sent out to Community members so that they can read them and prepare their responses before the EGM.

Please note that only registered Community members will be able to vote on any proposals for amendments and/or additions to the new constitution (to find out who can be a registered member please read Article III of our current constitution).

The date of the EGM will be announced after the above cut-off date.

With prayers and blessings,
+Bishop Raphael of Ilion <This message was edited>

27/05/2025, 17:08 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear Fathers and Community Members,

Christ is Risen!

Deeply humbled, I am sending you this message as the newly bestowed chairman of the Church Committee.

Please allow me to present you the new Committee members:

Irineos Livadiotes - Chair
Antigoni Mitsioni - Secretary
Aikaterina Douma - Treasurer
Dimitrios Erkekoglou
Andrew Evans
Maria Eugenicos
Alina Gavris
Carles George Ibanez
Eugenia Mitic
Janet Noakes
Mathaios Tsolakis

I am looking to work closely with all Committee members for the flourishing of our Community in God.

With the blessing and shepherding of our Bishop Raphael of Ilion, as a Community, we embarked on an important journey of development with the immediate focus being our premises at 2c Craigmillar Park and our transition as charity to a Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO). In the ensuing months, as Church Committee we will be focusing on these projects  along with all the little gears and big wheels turning in the background that make our Community an engaging, warm and welcoming place.

Taking a closer look to our SCIO constitution and following on from Bishop Raphael's message earlier in the month, we are looking to organise the Extraordinary General Meeting (EGM) to discuss the comments to be submitted by registered members (please email them to Antigoni mantigon@msn.com by Thursday 29/05 23.59). We intend to carefully consider every comment and recommendation we receive. The Committee appointed a working group to collate and prepare the comments to be presented to you ahead of the EGM which will be called once the comments are collated and, if required, undergo a legal review.

Our aim is to be keeping you posted on the developments of the SCIO constitution as the EGM to be called is not just a formality—it will be a milestone for our Community.

Last but not least, to foster open conversation and ensure everyone’s voice is heard, I will personally operate an “Open Door” session every Monday during June, immediately after the Supplicatory Canon (around 20.00pm) at 2 Meadow Lane. Whether you have a specific suggestion, a question about the SCIO constitution, willing to offer some volunteering time or simply wish to share your thoughts about our Community, this is for what these sessions will be for.

Yours in Risen Christ,
Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης
Irineos Livadiotes
Chair of the Community Committee
The Edinburgh Orthodox Community of Saint Andrew

PS: If you haven't received the above message by tonight please register on our mailing list here:

For the all who are to be received by Baptism or Chrismation on Saturday 07-06-2025 please get in touch to Antigoni (mantigon@msn.com) as soon as practicable and *no later than Tuesday 03/06/2025*. Antigoni will be asking you for specific information which are needed for the Certificate you will receive and these need time to be prepared.

17/06/2025, 22:14 - Irineos Livadiotes: Funeral of Father Alexander (Rory Williams)

Father Alexander (Rory Williams) died very peacefully at home, in the early hours of Wednesday June 11, 2025. 

Father Alexander’s funeral will take place on Tuesday July 1.  He will arrive at Dunblane Cathedral at 10.30 am and the service will begin at 11.30 am. 

Everyone is welcome to a light lunch at the Cathedral Hall after the burial (Dunblane Cemetery).

There will be a retiring collection on behalf of Médecins Sans Frontières.

09/07/2025, 21:41 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear Fathers and Community Members,

It is with a great joy that I am communicating with you to share some updates on our ongoing projects. With the blessing of Bishop Raphael of Ilion, we have been working intensively on two key projects:

A. Progressing our Craigmillar Park Church project. We have now submitted the planning application that is required for some of our necessary works.  The application is expected to be on the Council's Planning portal from Friday 11th of July. With the project taking a shape,  we will soon be focusing our fundraising efforts on this project. 

B. Our transition to a Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO) is now a step closer as OSCR, the Independent Scottish Charity Regulator, has accepted our Constitution as voted through at the AGM of the 11th of May 2025. We also have now our new Charity number. The full transition is not yet completed, it will take a few months, so we are still operating with our old Constitution till then.

We are actively following up the decision taken at the last AGM for a review of the SCIO Constitution with regards to the comments received by members. An Extraordinary General Meeting (EGM) will be called in due course in this regard and further details will be circulated when available. 

Last but not least, I would like to thank all the volunteers of our Community who tirelessly  contribute to the work of our Church in so many different ways for the Love of God. If you too would like to offer your skills, time and enthusiasm, please speak to myself or any other Committee member.

Thank you for your continued support and engagement. 

Yours in Christ,
Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης
Irineos Livadiotes
Chair of the Community Committee
The Edinburgh Orthodox Community of Saint Andrew

17/09/2025, 12:29 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear Fathers and Community Members,
I am glad to be inviting you to our forthcoming Community Gathering, which will take place on Sunday, 21st September 2025, from 13:00 onwards at Craigmillar Park Church Hall.

We have the pleasure of having Abbess Theokseni and nuns from the Chrysopegi Convent in Chania, Crete as well as some pilgrims with us on Sunday 21st of September 2025. This is a great opportunity to come together in fellowship and also benefit from a talk by abbess Theokseni on the previous Abbess of the Monastery, Theosemni.

Thanks to our Giannis Pantzikis and Alex Sykakis, our resident chefs who working together have established for us what we need to use our Craigmillar Park Church kitchen for a Community event we will have a buffet lunch for all attending. Giannis will be taking care of the food for us on this occasion. 

Schedule:
Arrive: 13.00pm onwards
Lunch served: 13.30pm
Talk, Q&A: 14.45pm-16.00pm
Start cleaning:17.00pm

As always, I would like to express my gratitude to our dedicated volunteers, whose labours and enthusiasm sustain our work and ministry. If you wish to contribute in any way, please feel free to speak to me or any member of the Committee. We will for sure need help in cleaning the hall after!

Thank you for your ongoing support. We look forward to seeing you at Craigmillar Park Church as we continue to grow and strengthen our community together.

With warm regards,
Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης
Irineos Livadiotes
Chair of the Community Committee
The Edinburgh Orthodox Community of Saint Andrew

Dear all, my name is Anthony and I am a second year German and History student at the University of Edinburgh. I am pleased to say that with the blessing of His Grace, Bishop Raphael, and the guidance of Fr Antonios, I'll be applying to re-establish the Edinburgh University Orthodox Student Society this semester. In order to do so we need at least 20 signing members, of which at least 15 need to be current students at the university of Edinburgh. If you are a student at UoE and would be interested in joining the society, or a student/recent ex-alumni from any other academic institution in Edinburgh, please email me with the subject matter "OrthoSoc"  at marcusamcdermott@gmail.com. Please include your names, phone numbers and student numbers. I have a meeting with EUSA this Monday the 29th, so the sooner you can do this, the better.

Thank you in advance.
Sincerely,
Anthony.

07/10/2025, 11:22 - Irineos Livadiotes: MEADOW LANE SECURITY MEASURES

Dear all,

On behalf of the Bishop and the Community Committee, we would like to draw your attention to observing some measures we have put in place for the use of Meadow Lane.

The door latch (Yale lock) of the main door is to be kept in the "lock" position at all times than 15 minutes before services, sacraments (including the Walk-in confession sessions) and events (including Youth Fellowship meetings). Separate arrangements are in place for Catechetical sessions, Sunday school and Greek School.

Outside these hours, the door is only opened using the interphone by authorised people including the Bishop, Priests, Committee members and authorised key holders.

Please *do not* open the door to check who is ringing or knocking but seek for one of the authorised people to do so.

Thank you for your cooperation on this matter.

With love in Christ,
St Andrews Community Committee

21/11/2025, 10:53 - Alina Gavriș: DUNBLANE CHURCH FUNDRAISING EVENT AND BAKING CONTRIBUTIONS: With the blessing of Bishop Raphael, this event will take place on the morning of Saturday 29th November at Dunblane Cathedral. Anyone who wishes to support the Dunblane Orthodox Church Community is invited along and baked cake contributions are welcome.
All baked items (they do not need to be fasting) can be brought to 2 Meadow Lane on Friday, 28th November, either before or after Vespers. Please label everything clearly “For Dunblane Coffee Morning” and include both the name of the baking and a list of ingredients.
All contributions will be taken to Dunblane early on Saturday morning by Fr Antonios.
If anyone is able to prepare Greek baklava or any other traditional national baking, it would be greatly appreciated by the Dunblane community.
21/11/2025, 12:50 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear Fathers and Community Members,

Many years for today’s Feast of the Entry of the Theotokos to the Temple.

I am writing to update you on two key developments in our Community: The building works at 2 Meadow Lane and our transition to SCIO.

With God's help, with generous donations from people with Christ in their hearts and the diligent work of our Bishop Raphael, Fr Antonios and Matthaios Tsolakis, we have now started some much needed maintenance works on the exterior of our premises at 2 Meadow Lane. 

The works started earlier in the week and last until about May 2026. These will include stone masonry, roof repairs and the replacement of specific windows.

During the works, scaffolding will be in place. Please avoid parking along the yellow line outside the building. Access to the building will be as usual. 

As all activities at 2 Meadow Lane will continue, we would like to draw the attention of all, especially families with children, to the importance of following the Health and Safety guidance to be given for within the building as well as and around the scaffolding on the perimeter. Notices will be posted to make the requirements clear. We kindly request that no bicycles are chained to the scaffolding as the pavement needs to remain clear as an accessible route for all. 

Also, as of Friday 14th of November we have effectively transitioned to a Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO) as our legal status. We will soon be sending more information on this including the constitution we are operating under and the process for you as individuals to become of becoming legal members of the SCIO.

Last but not least, I would like to wish to all a blessed and fruitful advent period to Christmas.

Thank you for your continued support and engagement, especially those who volunteer in all capacities.

In Christ,

Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης

Irineos Livadiotes

Chair of the Community Committee
The Edinburgh Orthodox Community of Saint Andrew, a 'Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation' (SCIO). Scottish Charity Number: SC054378

07/12/2025, 23:56 - Irineos Livadiotes: IMG-20251208-WA0000.jpg (file attached)
Christmas Carols Consert 09/12/2025

You are invited to the Christmas Carols consert of our Community!
When: Tuesday 09/12/2025. 19.00 - 20.30
Where: 2c Craigmillar Park Church EH16 5PG

The Orthodox School will have a small fundraising sale. Any donations of items or food are welcome. Please speak to Catherine Henderson for more details.

*Park and Stride to Craigmillar Park Church*
We want to be good to our neighbours and we would gratefully remind everyone that when travelling by car to and from Craigmillar Park  Church please remember to:

1. Minimise the number of car journeys by car sharing with others as much as possible.

2. Do *NOT* park in the following locations:
*A. In front of the Church.* We kindly ask this space is kept for people who need quick and step-free  access to the church and hall.

B. In front of the house of our next door neighbours on James Gall Wynd (the road to the north of the church hall). 

C. At any point on James Gall Wynd. This is a private road and parking without permit should be avoided. 

3. If you bring your car, please park it a little further away and STRIDE the final stretch.

4. Use active and public transport as much as possible.

24/12/2025, 12:36 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear all,

On behalf of the Community Committee I would like to wish each and everyone a blessed, joyous and peaceful Christmas. I wish and pray our hearts being filled with the gifts of Love, Fellowship and Charity for those not so fortunate. 

*Today is born of a Virgin, he who holds creation in the hollow of his hand.*
*Σήμερον γεννᾶται ἐκ Παρθένου, ὁ δρακὶ τὴν πᾶσαν ἔχων κτίσιν.*
(From the 9th Royal Hour of Christmas)

All the best wishes,

I am very pleased to announce that Fr Ilie Bobaianu, a very well-known Orthodox Christian iconographer (you may find Fr Ilie's works in the "Bizantes" Facebook page) has kindly accepted to run an iconography workshop for our Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew but interested people from other UK parishes are also welcome to participate. Places for this workshop are limited and those of you with a strong interest in pursuing iconography will be given priority. Please contact me in private if you would like to participate in this 6 days’ workshop. Thank you, Alina

1. Is this a family friendly event?
YES.

Yes, donation boxes on the day or using the link above.

Online until 20.00pm on Saturday 14/2. Any unsold tickets will be available at the door.

Yes you can get alcoholic, non-alcoholic and soft drinks at pop-up bar within the hall.

*Park and Stride to Craigmillar Park Church*
We want to be good to our neighbours and we would gratefully remind everyone that when travelling by car to and from Craigmillar Park Church please remember to:

1. Minimise the number of car journeys by car sharing with others as much as possible.

2. Do *NOT* park in the following locations:
*A. In front of the Church.* We kindly ask this space is kept for people who need quick and step-free  access to the church and hall.

B. In front of the house of our next door neighbours on James Gall Wynd (the road to the north of the church hall). 

C. At any point on James Gall Wynd. This is a private road and parking without permit should be avoided. 

3. If you bring your car, please park it a little further away and STRIDE the final stretch.

4. Use active and public transport as much as possible.

https://www.eventbrite.co.uk/e/meatfare-fundraiser-party-tickets-1981541976393?aff=oddtdtcreator

14/03/2026, 18:24 - Irineos Livadiotes: Dear Fathers, Brothers and Sisters in Christ, 
 
Please read till the end. If anything does not seem clear, feel free to speak to us in person.
 
This is an email inviting you to become registered legal members of our newly formed Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO) which is the legal entity of the Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew (SC054378). 
 
Being a registered legal member means you are able to:
- Participate and vote in Annual General Meetings (AGMs) and Special General Meetings (SGMs)
- Vote for or be voted as Committee members. 
 
To save you filling different forms, we have incorporated the various forms (Membership, Newsletter, Gift Aid) into one. Please choose all that apply where you are prompted.
*We prefer if you fill this form online*, yet the form will also be available in paper format in the coming weeks at the desk by the entrance to the Chapel St church. Please return the filled-in form to the secretary of the Community Committee Antigoni Mitsioni.
 
Application form: https://forms.office.com/pages/responsepage.aspx?id=vPEYq317pk6fUNweLEkwAvXUGwYznFRHrgDvDXxHsj5UMlpXU0JURVZINFRYVVVXTEFUTjlaQ1dGNy4u&route=shorturl

Please read all the information on the application form, as this membership differs from our previous arrangements for certain legal reasons. There is still the requirement for you to be approved as a registered legal member although we have no membership fees. As per our Constitution the Community Committee will review each application and approve all those who fulfil the eligibility criteria. We will communicate with you to confirm the Committee's Decision. We aim to do this as soon as possible but it may take up to 60 days to provide a response.
 
*This form needs to be completed by all including those who are registered members of the old charity (SC040613).* The first members of the newly formed SCIO were the 127 members whom we had as registered legal members of the old charity at the time we submitted our application to the Office of the Scottish Charity Regulator (OSCR) in June 2025. We are asking everybody, including registered members, to fill in the form as a way of updating the information we hold for you and as a re-registration process under the terms of article 18 of our ​​Constitution​​ (https://shorturl.at/HHmok), which will allow you to confirm that you wish to remain legal members of the Community. If we do not receive a filled-in form or written confirmation that you wish to be a member of the Community within 28 days from today, the Committee may decide that your membership will not continue and the application form will need to be completed to register you again as a legal member.
As a charitable organisation we have a duty to inform the Office of the Scottish Charity Regulator (OSCR) about the SCIO's members. 
According to OSCR's regulations, if we are asked, we need to send a list to OSCR with the names and addresses of the voting members of our newly formed SCIO. By becoming member you are authorising the Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew to share your name and address with OSCR.
 
Please note that our AGM published via our schedule of Church services for Sunday 10/05/2026 at 13.15 will be under our SCIO constitution and will be open for the registered legal members who completed their application by midnight of 20/04/2026.
 
Many thanks,

19/03/2026, 10:38 - Irineos Livadiotes: Thoughts on the Fourth Sunday of the Fast by his Eminence Archbishop Nikitas

A few people asked me to continue sharing my thoughts/comments about the lenten Sundays. So, here we go.

Allow me to make a few introductory comments about the Fourth Sunday of Lent. If one reads the Gospel (Mark 9: 17-31), he/she sees that there are two critical issues.

1. Christ, again, is shown as the one who heals. He heals the boy who "has a dumb spirit". The disciples failed to do anything and Christ tells them that both fasting and prayer are needed (the great two disciplines) to drive out the spirit from the boy.

2. Christ already speaks to the disciples and tells of His crucifixion and resurrection. We are being prepared for what is to come - the Cross, the Tomb and the Resurrection. "The Son of man will be delivered into the hands of men, and they will kill him; and when he is killed, after three days he will rise."

Over the years, as we have seen in the other Sundays, a "new" meaning/dedication was given to the say. In this case, the Fourth Sunday of Lent is now in honor of St. John of the Ladder, who struggled a whole lifetime on Mount Sinai. During his life, he wrote the Klimax, the Ladder. It is a text for monastics, although many people living in the world read and study this divinely-inspired work. As Moses gave the tablets of the law to the people of Israel, St. John had given us this heavenly book. I would like to share a few lines from the Ladder with you. I pray you find wisdom in his writings.

I have seen men who were going to steal and were not afraid of God, but, hearing the barking of dogs, they at once turned back; and what the fear of God could not achieve was done by the fear of animals.

The silence of Jesus put Pilate to shame.

Let your prayer be completely simple. For both the publican and the prodigal son were reconciled to God by a single phrase.

Do not try to be verbose when you pray, lest your mind be distracted in searching for words. One word of the publican propitiated God, and one cry of faith saved the thief. Loquacity in prayer often distracts the mind and leads to phantasy, whereas brevity makes concentration.

A horse when alone often imagines that it is galloping, but when it is with others it finds out how slow it is.

27/03/2026, 13:35 - Irineos Livadiotes: Some thoughts on the Fifth Sunday of Lent from His Eminence Archbishop Nikitas.

By the grace and mercy of God, we will arrive at the Fifth Sunday of Holy and Great Lent this weekend. For Eastern Orthodox Christians, Holy week is not part of Lent. The Saturday of Lazarus and Holy week form a new entity and they guide and lead us into Pascha.

The readings of the day (Hebrews 9: 11-14 and Mark 10: 32-45) give us an understanding of how the early Church used the pericopes as teaching tools. The Epistle Reading speaks of the sacrifice of old, that according to the Law. We read that the blood of animals was offered, as directed by the Law. But we also read that the blood of Christ was offered (by himself) to redeem us from sin and death. His sacrifice has redeemed us for eternity. The Gospel passage, like that of the previous Sunday, is one that prepares us for the Passion, Crucifixion and Resurrection. The reading also reminds us that "the Son of Man" came to serve and not be served. He came to freely offer Himself for the life of the world.

While the Church keeps these readings on the Sunday, she has added another commemoration to her calendar. On the Fifth Sunday of Lent, we remember and commemorate St. Mary of Egypt. Her life is written and offered to us by St. Sophronios the great Patriarch of Jerusalem. Her life is read as part of the Great Canon, which is read/chanted during the fifth week of the Great Fast. St. Mary stands as a model of repentance and by her witness we see the forgiving grace and love of God. Perhaps, it is for this reason that St. Sophronios begins the life with the words - "it is good to hide the secret of a king but glorious to reveal the works of God". The rational mind may find it difficult to understand how the Church has named her a Saint - but, when one reads her life and understands the true meaning of repentance and the forgiving love of God, he/she can see why the Church has placed this feast at the end of the lenten season.

The last hymn of the Orthros Service also speaks to the faithful and instructs us. The hymnographer writes - "The Kingdom of God is not food and drink, but righteousness and abstinence with holiness . The righteous man shows mercy all the day long; his delight is in the Lord, and walking in the light he shall not stumble". Let us remember these words, especially when we think and speak of the Fast.

May we reach Pascha and repent, as did St. Mary, so we may also be forgiven by our Lord.

30/03/2026, 16:14 - Irineos Livadiotes: RESHARING AT THE REQUEST OF SOME PARISHIONERS 

Dear Fathers, Brothers and Sisters in Christ, 
 
Please read till the end. If anything does not seem clear, feel free to speak to us in person.
 
This is a message inviting you to become registered legal members of our newly formed Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation (SCIO) which is the legal entity of the Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew (SC054378). 
 
Being a registered legal member means you are able to:
- Participate and vote in Annual General Meetings (AGMs) and Special General Meetings (SGMs)
- Vote for or be voted as Committee members. 
 
To save you filling different forms, we have incorporated the various forms (Membership, Newsletter, Gift Aid) into one. Please choose all that apply where you are prompted.
*We prefer if you fill this form online*, yet the form will also be available in paper format in the coming weeks at the desk by the entrance to the Chapel St church. Please return the filled-in form to the secretary of the Community Committee Antigoni Mitsioni.
 
Application form: https://forms.office.com/pages/responsepage.aspx?id=vPEYq317pk6fUNweLEkwAvXUGwYznFRHrgDvDXxHsj5UMlpXU0JURVZINFRYVVVXTEFUTjlaQ1dGNy4u&route=shorturl

Please read all the information on the application form, as this membership differs from our previous arrangements for certain legal reasons. There is still the requirement for you to be approved as a registered legal member although we have no membership fees. As per our Constitution the Community Committee will review each application and approve all those who fulfil the eligibility criteria. We will communicate with you to confirm the Committee's Decision. We aim to do this as soon as possible but it may take up to 60 days to provide a response.
 
*This form needs to be completed by all including those who are registered members of the old charity (SC040613).* The first members of the newly formed SCIO were the 127 members whom we had as registered legal members of the old charity at the time we submitted our application to the Office of the Scottish Charity Regulator (OSCR) in June 2025. We are asking everybody, including registered members, to fill in the form as a way of updating the information we hold for you and as a re-registration process under the terms of article 18 of our ​​Constitution​​ (https://shorturl.at/HHmok), which will allow you to confirm that you wish to remain legal members of the Community. If we do not receive a filled-in form or written confirmation that you wish to be a member of the Community within 28 days from today, the Committee may decide that your membership will not continue and the application form will need to be completed to register you again as a legal member.
As a charitable organisation we have a duty to inform the Office of the Scottish Charity Regulator (OSCR) about the SCIO's members. 
According to OSCR's regulations, if we are asked, we need to send a list to OSCR with the names and addresses of the voting members of our newly formed SCIO. By becoming member you are authorising the Edinburgh Orthodox Community of St Andrew to share your name and address with OSCR.
 
Please note that our AGM published via our schedule of Church services for Sunday 10/05/2026 at 13.15 will be under our SCIO constitution and will be open for the registered legal members who completed their application by midnight of 20/04/2026.
 
Many thanks,
Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης
Irineos Livadiotes
Chair of the Community Committee
The Edinburgh Orthodox Community of Saint Andrew, a 'Scottish Charitable Incorporated Organisation' (SCIO). Scottish Charity Number: SC054378

09/04/2026, 17:38 - Irineos Livadiotes: *Important safety advice*
Great Week and Pascha 2026:  

As we are now approaching some of the most popular services of the year, we are putting in place some measures to accommodate our church services as safely as possible. 

1. Stewards in high-visibility jackets will be on duty to ensure people are in the church safely and all exit corridors are maintained clean and accessible. They are the only people fully informed and authorised to make safety decisions. We are very grateful that they have volunteered to look after us. Please do as they ask you. 
2. There is a limit on numbers inside the church because of fire safety regulations. At peak times you may have to wait until you can go inside. 
3. We must keep a clear pathway all the way across the church between the two exits on either side. Please do not stand or sit in this pathway, and move if a steward requests it. No prams, or bulky items to be kept along those pathways especially close to the doors.
4. Families with young children in prams and people with reduced mobility will be given priority space. Please ask one of the stewards. 
5. Be extra careful outside as the ground is uneven and there are tripping hazards. 
6. In an emergency please follow the directions given by the stewards.
7. Should the church be at capacity, stewards will guide people who cannot be accommodated inside the church for lighting a candle and venerate. 
8. First aiders are also on duty. If you or someone next to you needs first aid, please reach out immediately to Antigoni (main entrance) or Dr Maria.

16/04/2026, 15:37 - Irineos Livadiotes: *Annual General Meeting on Sunday 10th May 2026*

Dear fathers, brothers and sisters in Christ,
CHRIST IS RISEN!
We would like to invite you to our Annual General Meeting on Sunday 10th May 2026 at 13:15 pm at 33 Chapel Street.
The AGM is open for all registered legal members to fully participate and vote yet all who worship in our church and Community are welcome to attend. We would encourage all of you to join us and make your voice heard on matters of our Community.
Should you like to register as legal member please follow the instructions found here ( https://mailchi.mp/bf1ff039d159/a-letter-to-parishioners-from-fr-raphael-11044654?e=76c448196a) and make sure you submit your application form by 20/04/2026 to ensure you can participate and vote at this year's AGM. Thank you to those we have submitted their application form already. We will make sure you get a response to your application before the AGM.
Here is the planned agenda:
Agenda:

Prayer

Introduction

Reports:
a) Clergy (Bishop Raphael)
b) Community committee (Irineos Livadiotes)
c) Treasurer (Aikaterini Douma)
d) Sunday School (Presvytera Marion)
e) Greek School (Eirini Theofanidou)
f) Youth fellowship - COTY Edinburgh (Fr Antonios / Eleana Bozini)

Update on Craigmillar Park Church (Bishop Raphael/Irineos Livadiotes)

SCIO constitution amends (Irineos Livadiotes/Dimitrios Erkekoglou)

Minutes of the last AGM

Any other business

Prayer

All items include an initial presentation by the person in charge and time at the end of each for comments, questions and answers.

With warm prayers in Resurrected Christ,

Many thanks,
Ειρηναίος Λειβαδιώτης
Irineos Livadiotes
Chair of the Community Committee <This message was edited>

Thursday 30/4 at 19.30(after vespers) at Meadow Lane

The Resilient Christian
How to manage stress, anxiety & panic attacks,
By Fay Kalapoda, Accredited CBT and EMDR therapist
This Psychological Wellbeing Talk will cover Cognitive Behavioural Therapy’s evidence-based theory and top tips and will cover:

• The Fight or Flight response
• How to identify your stress triggers
• Cognitive techniques and attention retraining to quieten the mind
• Breathing retraining and progressive muscle relaxation to calm down the body
• Other emotion regulation and behavioural strategies

 *Please note this workshop purely convers psychological theory and tips and will not be covering theological concepts on the topic.

Thursday 07/5 at 21.00 Vigil for the feast of St John the Theologian at Meadow Lane. Vespers, Matins and Divine Liturgy.

Thursday 14/5 at 19.30(after vespers) at Meadow Lane
From an Open House in Brighton to an Orthodox Parish in Keswick,
Guest speaker: Presvytera Jennifer Bega Musther

05/05/2026, 15:49 - Antigoni: Forwarded: 

Dear UoE OrthoSoc Members,


This is a reminder that our *Annual General Meeting (AGM)* will take place *tomorrow, 6 May at 18:00* in the *Lomond Room, Pleasance*. (60 Pleasance, Edinburgh EH8 9TJ)

The meeting will be held in a *hybrid format*, meaning you are welcome to attend either in person or *online*. 

To join online please use the following link: https://teams.microsoft.com/l/message/19:f8lzK8aJwKsJfH_3YMWg35JKc_VbevoPYusyxGgjSpY1@thread.tacv2/1777988591119?tenantId=2e9f06b0-1669-4589-8789-10a06934dc61&groupId=71bb4231-2180-4833-adcf-218fe3f9d25e&parentMessageId=1777988591119&teamName=OrthoSoc%20AGM&channelName=General&createdTime=1777988591119

The AGM will cover key matters including:

* Review of the past year
* Election of all Office Bearer roles for the coming year
* Consideration and voting on any motion properly submitted in advance 
* Presentation of the current financial position of the Society, including committed expenditure and budget
* Any other competent business in accordance with the Constitution

Please note that *only members who are officially registered as members of the Society through EUSA are eligible to vote and to stand for election*.

If you wish to submit a motion for discussion, or if you intend to stand for a committee position, please contact the Secretary in advance at A.S.Powell@sms.ed.ac.uk. 


Your participation is important, and we strongly encourage you to attend and help shape the future of the Society.

Kind regards,
Ariel Powell
Secretary
UoE OrthoSoc

14/05/2026, 13:08 - Carles Ibanez: <Media omitted>
We (Presvytera Georgina, Ambrose-Robin and I) are planning the care of the *garden at Craigmillar Park Church* to get ready for when we start using the Church. This consists of the lawn and the plants in the front garden, planting along the other side of the church, and raised beds at the back. Ambrose Robin (who is a professional gardener) is drawing up a landscaping proposal to present to the Bishop and committee by the autumn. We need the help of gardener-parishioners:
 
- We would welcome any *offers of seeds, bulbs or plants.*

- also, *volunteer gardeners* are needed! This is a chance (perhaps for someone without a garden of their own) to adopt a planting space in line with the landscaping plan, and in return (as well as looking after their own patch) to do some weeding in the main garden.

Please speak to any of us if you can offer plants or help.
